# Fortress gap ledger

The gap ledger records, one claim to a row, where the revival's tree departs from Fortress as its specification and the team's papers describe it: what fails, what works that a source doubted, and what a source claimed wrongly. Each row carries its status, its kind and area, the specification's word on it, a reproducer, and who found it; the open rows are the bug reports that the work fixes. A row keeps its number for good, wherever it sits: a fixed or repeated row becomes one line in its place, and the full earlier line of every changed row is in `explorations/fortress-gap-ledger-history.md`.

Read a row with `explorations/coordinator/tools/facts-extract.sh 'ledger:N'`, and find rows with `explorations/coordinator/tools/facts-extract.sh 'ledger-find:WORDS'`; do not read this file whole. Write it only with `explorations/coordinator/tools/ledger.py` (`add FILE --section TITLE`, `note`, `rewrite N FILE`, `close`, `duplicate`), which checks every line it writes against the template below and keeps the history; `ledger.py count` prints the counts by status, kind and area, `ledger.py check` lints the whole file, and `ledger.py landing-check BASE` runs the checks of a landing since BASE.

## The row template

A row is one line of eight cells, `| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |`, at most 1,200 characters, in the table of its topic section, in number order. A pipe inside a cell is written `\|`. (POSITIONS, "The gap ledger's form"; the proposal behind it is `explorations/coordinator/process-engineering/gap-ledger-archaeology.md`, section 4.)

- **#**: the row's number, the next free one when it is added; never reused, renumbered or given to another row. 148 is vacant.
- **claim**: at most 300 characters, whose first 150 name the defect or the capability: what fails or what holds, and where.
- **status**: exactly one word of seven, with no second status and nothing in brackets:
  - `POSITIVE-VERIFIED`: it works; it ran and its output is recorded.
  - `NEGATIVE-VERIFIED`: the specification settles it and the reproducer confirms it. A row with one half fixed stays `NEGATIVE-VERIFIED`, and its notes say which half is fixed.
  - `NEGATIVE-BOUNDED`: the failure reproduces; the mechanisms tried are named and are not exhaustive.
  - `CONTESTED`: unsettled; the notes say why.
  - `RETIRED`: a source's claim falsified here, kept so that it is not derived again.
  - `FIXED`: closed by a landed commit (`ledger.py close`): the claim's first sentence, the test as reproducer, notes `fixed HASH; full text: history, row N`.
  - `DUPLICATE`: a pointer (`ledger.py duplicate`), notes `duplicate of row M; full text: history, row N`.
- **class**: `kind (area)`; `—` only for `POSITIVE-VERIFIED` and `RETIRED`. The kind is one of `implementation gap`, `library gap vs spec`, `library bug`, `design limit`, `deliberate`, `typesetter`, `packaging`; the area one of `parser`, `walk`, `checker`, `codegen`, `runtime`, `library`, `prelude`, `specification`, `tests`, `tools`.
- **spec citation**: the `.tex` file and the section heading in quotes, as `basic/operators/juxtameaning.tex`, "Juxtaposition"; a line number only against `Specification-1.0-frozen/`; `silent` where the specification does not say.
- **reproducer**: one test (its name, or its path under the test folders; an `XXX` test while it is an expected failure) or one probe path from the repository root, at most 200 characters; `none` when nothing in the tree reproduces it, with the command and its output, or the probe's old path, in the notes.
- **found by**: the stream that found it (`climb batch 8 rung I`, `run-c3`, `apl rung 2`), at most 60 characters; no date and no role.
- **notes / workaround**: at most 700 characters: `siblings: N, M` first if there are any; the mechanism, with file:line and the commit it was true at; the workaround; for a closed row, `fixed HASH`.

A path under `explorations/` that is no longer in the tree is read from history: `git show $(git log -1 --diff-filter=D --format=%h -- PATH)^:PATH`.

## 1. Syntax: lexing, expressions and declarations

The parser and walk's reading of program text: tokens, operator words and reserved words, subscripts and superscripts after a primary, and the declaration forms with their naming and shadowing rules.

| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `x[i][j]` and `x[i]^2` are grammatical, but walk invokes a subscript followed by a subscript or a superscript without its first index: one index fails with `args = ()`, more silently give the wrong element | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (walk) | `appendices/grammars/concrete-syntax.tex`, "Expressions"; `basic/operators/precedence.tex`, "Operator Precedence and Associativity"; `basic/operators/juxtameaning.tex`, "Juxtaposition" | none | astra, blinded, review | siblings: 84. Such a primary parses as a `MathPrimary` (not `SubscriptExpr`); its subscript goes to `evalAndInvokeMethod` (`interpreter/evaluator/Evaluator.java:1181`), whose `evalInvocationArgs` (`:1064`, `:1380-1381`) takes `exprs.subList(1,…)`, right for a juxtaposition list, wrong for a subscript list. `m[3,7]^2` prints `11449`, `(m[7])^2`, not `1075369` (probe `explorations/gap-ledger-probes/g02.fss`, in the parent of `e879b6a44`). The spec forbids only `x^2[i]`. Workarounds: `(x[i])[j]`, `(x[i])^2`, or bind first, as `explorations/apl/base/AplCore.fss:1859` does (`rk = rs[k]`). probe and full text: history, row 1 |

| 5 | U+2211 `∑` lexes as a prefix operator, never as an accumulator; only ASCII `SUM` is a big operator | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (parser) | `basic/expressions/reductions.tex`, "Summations and Other Reduction Expressions" (`:20`, `Accumulator ::= $\sum$ ∣ $\prod$ ∣ BIG (Encloser ∣ Op)`) | none | ours | `Operator prefix SUM is not defined.` The grammar's `Accumulator` takes the keyword `SUM`, which matches the ASCII letters only, and has no `∑` alternative (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/parser/Symbol.rats:252`, `:260-261`), so `∑`, an operator named `SUM` (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/parser_util/precedence_resolver/Operators.java:2082`), is read as a prefix operator. Fortify renders ASCII `SUM` as `Σ`, so the *rendered* form is unaffected. Workaround: write `SUM`. Probe `explorations/sum-probes/p13_unicode_sigma.fss`, in the parent of `e879b6a44`. |

| 924 | the parser reads one 16-bit unit as the character of a character literal, so a character past U+FFFF written as itself is refused, alone, `'😀'`, or followed by a space and its code, `'😀 1f600'`: "Syntax Error"; its code alone, `'1f600'`, reads 128512 | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (parser) | `basic/lexical-structure.tex`, "Character Literals" | XXXCharLiteralSupplementary | climb batch 27 | The single-character and redundant choices of `CharLiteralContent` take `a1:(!InvalidCharLiteralContent _)`, one Java `char` (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/parser/Literal.rats:447`, `:453`), so a supplementary character's high surrogate is read alone and the low one stands where the space or the closing mark must be. ## 2. Types: generics, static parameters, inference and coercion

Type parameters and nat (size) parameters, how walk and the compiled checker infer and bind them, their run-time descriptors on the compiled path, dimensions, and coercion.

The cap is `combos.size > 64` (`scala_src/typechecker/impls/Functionals.scala:394` at fa13e6383c). A lone parameter that numerals alone fix takes their default, its one choice, so `g3(1, 2, 3, 4)` is accepted. A `ZZ32` argument has four choices on the prelude (none, `ZZ32`, `ZZ64`, `ZZ`), so three such parameters make 64 and pass, and four make 256. The choices of independent lone parameters could be made one parameter at a time, except that the expected type couples them (`context` in `constraint`). ## 4. Operators and overloading under walk

User operator declarations and fixity (the APL glyphs among them), overload families over the library's arrays and the Meet Rule as walk applies it, and walk's overload check at load.

| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 29 | multifix dispatch is absent: walk never looks for an *n*-argument definition of an infix operator repeated between three or more operands, and reassociates to binary calls | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (walk) | `Specification/basic/operators/chained-multifix.tex`, "Chained and Multifix Operators"; `Specification-1.0-frozen/fortress/preamble.tex:59` (multifix among the not-yet-supported features) | none | ours | Walk applies an operator of more than two operands pairwise, left to right, unless it is enclosing (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/interpreter/evaluator/Evaluator.java:994`). `a OTIMES b OTIMES c = V(24.0, n=2)`: the right value with two children, so two binary calls (probe `explorations/spec-probes/p13_multifix2.fss`, in the parent of e879b6a44). A tight juxtaposition of three non-function items with a ternary `opr juxtaposition` declared runs the binary ones too: `a(b)(c)` gives `123` where the compiled path's multifix gives `1023`, for `K(10 a.i + b.i)` and `K(900 + 100 a.i + 10 b.i + c.i)` (at a1a75716a). Workaround: a reduction over a concatenation monoid. |

## 6. Integer arithmetic

The fixed-width and unbounded integer types on both paths: overflow and wrapping, division and its relatives, powers, shifts, narrowing and widening, signed and unsigned.

## 7. Numerals, floats and comparisons

What a numeral denotes on each path, the float types, floor, ceiling and rounding, rationals, and total comparisons, equality, MIN and MAX.

| 94 | the spec's constant `pi` is not in the default library: it is a `FloatLiteral` in the optional `Constants` API, not the spec's `RationalValueTimesPi` object, and the Unicode name is undefined | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | library gap vs spec (library) | `Specification/basic/expressions/literals.tex`, "Pi" | none | run-b | siblings: 95. Bare: `Variable pi is not defined.`; the Unicode spelling: `Variable ? is not defined.` (the interpreter cannot echo the character). Workaround: `import Constants.{pi}` (`Library/Constants.fss:15`): `pi = 3.141592653589793`, `pi r^2 = 12.566370614359172`. Probes `explorations/run-b/probes/worker/w06_pi_bare.fss`, `w06_pi_unicode.fss` (fail), `w06_pi.fss` and `explorations/run-b/probes/c21_constants_pi.fss` (work), in the parent of ed6dafb52. full text: history, row 94 |

The fold writes a negative result with a radix point as prefix minus on a numeral, and a quotient as `n / d`, reusing an operator reference written in the component, whose overloadings the disambiguator gave (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/compiler/desugarer/IntegerLiteralFoldingVisitor.java`, `forComponent`, at 8e4ca7b070); with none it leaves the expression. It also leaves `p.0 / d` with `p` or `d` beyond 2^53, a power whose exponent times its base's bits passes 65536, and a `CHOOSE` with `min(k, n - k)` beyond 4096 or `n` beyond 64 bits. Both paths. Workaround: a prefix minus written anywhere in the component. |

A site whose type names a type parameter of its generic declaration, as `m(): Gen[\T\] = 3` in `object Box[\T\]`, has no one class to hold its object. Workaround: bind the value once, at a top-level variable. |

The prelude declares `trait IntLiteral` and `trait FloatLiteral` (`LibraryBuiltin/CompilerBuiltin.fss:883`, `:996` at 88d5d260e4), and the checker types a numeral by them under the prelude (`compiler/Types.java`, F-79). The code generator calls a program's coercion once per site in the component class's static initializer, and at a generic type at each evaluation (row 957). Workaround: none. |

## 8. Generators, reductions and ranges

Big operators and reductions, the generator protocol, the typing of the library's reduction devices and generic collections, and ranges.

| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The nullary + `BigOperator` + `__bigOperatorSugar` protocol is the library's invention; the specification never describes it, and declares a big operator "as a usual operator declaration". ## 9. Arrays, vectors and matrices

Array literals, pasting, comprehensions and factories, and the library's Vector, Matrix and rank-3 arrays: their algebra, views, subscripts and assignment.

| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 35 | the library ships none of the three matrix operators the specification promises: transpose `M^T`, power `M^k` and norm `‖M‖` | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | library gap vs spec (library) | `Specification-1.0-frozen/basic/operators/opr-overview.tex:56-57, 75-81, 87-91`, "Enclosing Operators", "Exponentiation", "Superscript Operators" | none | ours | The trait `Matrix` declares `+`, `-`, `scale`, `mul`, `rmul`, `lmul` and the method `t()`, and no `^` and no norm (`Library/FortressLibrary.fsi:1757-1770` at c124c6b65); the only norm is the vector's (`Library/FortressLibrary.fsi:1706`). The errors under walk, in that order: `Operator postfix ^T is not defined.`; `Failed to find any matching overload … (__DefaultMatrix,2)`; `Cannot unify __DefaultMatrix with Vector[\T,k\]`. Workaround: `M.t()` for the transpose, the product `M M` for a power. Probes `explorations/libvector-probes/p25_spec_gaps.fss` and `explorations/libvector-probes/p26_matrix_norm.fss`, in the parent of `e879b6a44`. |
| 49 | an array literal with no declared type is not built into an array under walk: an untyped binding fails with `Can't infer element type for array construction`, and a literal in argument position is passed on as a paste, an `IUOTuple` never built into an array | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (walk) | `Specification/basic/expressions/aggregate.tex`, "Array Expressions" | `ProjectFortress/tests/XXXArrayLiteralArgRungC.fss` | ours, run-c3, climb batch 3 rung C | siblings: 104, 142. `LHSEvaluator.forLValue` builds the array only from a declared type; with none it reports the error, the join of the element types left as a comment (`LHSEvaluator.java:140-145`, at c124c6b65). `Evaluator.forArrayElements` returns the paste, an `IUOTuple` (`Evaluator.java:854-861`), so `3 - [1 2]` fails dispatch with `(1,2): (ZZ32,ZZ32)`. `RR64` elements fail as a user type's do. ## 10. Strings, maps and printing

Strings and their operators, maps and their big operators, and how each path renders a value as text.

| 134 | `Map.union(f, other)` with a three-argument combining function works, including over matrix-valued maps; bare `UNION` keeps the left value | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | — | silent | none | run-b2 | The overlapping key holds the matrix sum, non-overlapping keys pass through, `\|u\| = 3`. The combiner is `(k: String, a: T, b: T) -> T` (`Library/Map.fsi:66`) — the key is passed and may be ignored. A fold of `union` over a list of maps sums many gradient environments, which is how run-b2 builds a parameter record around row 96. Probe `explorations/run-b2/probes/g1e_union.fss`, in the parent of `22ea0cd90`. |

## 11. Parallelism, exceptions and control flow

Implicit parallelism, spawn, atomic and transactions, and the control forms: try and catch, typecase, case, if with a generator, label and exit.

| 322 | an `atomic do … end` block as the trailing expression of a `do … also` arm produces a class that fails verification on the compiled path: `VerifyError: Inconsistent stackmap frames at branch target 5` | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (codegen) | `Specification/basic/expressions/also.tex`, "Parallel Do Expressions"; `Specification/basic/expressions/atomic.tex`, "Atomic Expressions" | none | ours | siblings: 526. `CodeGen.generateTaskCompute` keeps `this` on the operand stack across the arm's body for the result's `PUTFIELD` (`CodeGen.java:5028-5036`); `CodeGen.forAtomicBlock` branches from its handler, which clears the stack, back to `startOver`, its first label (`:1736-1764`). ## 12. The compiled path: constructs, crashes and the switch-over

What the code generator cannot compile and where the compiled checker crashes, the compiler's own library and prelude against the interpreter's, and the one library's declarations that the checker or the specification refuses.

## 13. Syntax extension: grammars, templates and sub-languages

User grammars declared in apis, the preparser, templates and gaps, hygiene and expansion errors, compiling a grammar, and the APL sub-language built on them.

| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 179 | whitespace in a grammar production is optional at the use site: a literal space between symbols and an explicit `SPACE` both translate to the nonterminal `w` | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | — | silent | none | apl rung 1 | `nospace⦇5,5⦈` and `nospace⦇ 5 , 5 ⦈` both print `10`, which lets the book's `2 4⍴⍳8` parse against a production written with spaces. `Syntax.rats:343-345` lexes `SPACE`; `ComposingSyntaxDefTranslator.java:147` maps it and a literal space to `w`. The converse is the limit: a sub-grammar cannot require whitespace, so APL strand notation rests on the greediness of `LiteralExpr`, not on `SPACE`. Verified on the retired first library, in a worktree of `0ff5eae27~1`; probe `explorations/apl/v1-probes/p02b_mech.fss`, in the parent of 1ee3b0bc5. full text: history, row 179 |
| 180 | one production serves `f/` for **every** function glyph: a glyph-table gap followed by the reduce slash, and the same with `⌿` | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | — | silent | none | apl rung 1 | `redop⦇ +/ 3 ⦈`, `⍳/`, `*/` and `redfi⦇ +⌿ 3 ⦈` all reduce to one call with the glyph's name, so APL's reduce is one rule, not one per glyph, though the `?`-bound gap that would make the slash optional is unimplemented (row 274). Measured on the retired first library; probe `explorations/apl/v1-probes/p02b_mech.fss`, gone. The current base writes one rule per glyph (`explorations/apl/base/AplSyntax.fsi:419-422`). full text: history, row 180 |
| 181 | the preparser reads a backtick escape in a grammar production as an opening quote wanting a closing `'` and rejects the api; the macro language's own special characters, APL's `+` and `*` among them, need that escape | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (parser) | silent | none | apl rung 1 | siblings: 211. `Unmatched delimiter "api".` and ``Unmatched delimiters "`" and "end".`` `PreCompilation.rats:115` puts the backtick in `Opening`; `PreParserState.java:256` pairs it only with `'` or `’`. Inside `⦇ ⦈` it also breaks their pairing. Workaround: an APL glyph above the escape: the preparser cannot tokenise it, its token loop (`PreCompilation.rats:56`, no end-of-file anchor) stops, and `report()` never runs; `explorations/apl/base/AplSyntax.fsi` keeps `⍝` (`:44`) above its first escape (`` `: ``, `:107`). Probes `explorations/apl/v1-probes/p08a_g.fsi` (fails), `p08b_g.fsi` (works), `p08d_g.fsi`, in the parent of 1ee3b0bc5. full text: history, row 181 |

| 183 | an **undelimited** host `Expr` gap swallows any following terminal the host knows as an operator (`≡`, `⋄`, `∘`), because a gap is greedy and Rats never re-enters a nonterminal for a shorter match | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | design limit (parser) | silent | none | apl rung 1 | siblings: 235. `esc2⦇ v ≡ m ⦈` makes the expander unparsable (`Variable esc2 is not defined`); `apl⦇ ⍎v ≡ 1 4⍴⍎v ⦈` parsed as `aplDy("rho", v EQV (1 4), v)`. | 757 | `fortress compile` of a file after `fortress typecheck` of the same unchanged file writes no jar and exits 0, so `fortress run` of it stops with 'Could not load P' and 'Resource not found : P.class' | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (tools) | silent | none | climb batch 17 rung A | On the base 3aea3da53, with a private caches folder: `explorations/coordinator/tools/old-fortress.sh <base> <caches> typecheck` of a probe SkTcThenCompile.fss, then `compile` of it (exit 0, no SkTcThenCompile.jar in the caches' bytecode_cache), then `run`; the probe was never committed. By reading, at c124c6b65: `repository/GraphRepository.java:283` takes a component whose analyzed_cache entry is no older than its source as done, and `isStale` (`:614`) compares source dates, its dependencies' included, with the cache's and checks a foreign change, never the jar. A compile of a fresh copy of the file under another name writes its jar. Workaround: compile before any typecheck of the file. |

So `Decls.scala:139-142`'s contract check never runs, and row 701's refusal of a contract is never reached. |

## 15. Performance and program shape

Measured costs of walk and the compiled path, and of the shapes a program can take on them.

| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 139 | the functional-backprop shape (every node carries a map from its cotangent to the parameter cotangents, sharing summed at fan-out) is exponential in the number of fan-outs along a chain; a tape is linear | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | — | silent | none | run-b | Not a Fortress defect: it is why run-b's engine is a tape, and the two shapes cannot be told apart at depth 1. At residual depths 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10: functional 79 / 70 / 197 / 400 / 1617 / 6587 ms per forward+backward, tape 105 / 41 / 62 / 84 / 72 / 77 ms (run-b measured 45/45/135/481/1872/6950 against 53/32/46/75/68/198). Probes `explorations/run-b/probes/s03_functional_depth.fss`, `s03_tape_depth.fss`, in the parent of `ed6dafb52`; full text: history, row 139 |
| 154 | the library matrix product costs tens of microseconds per multiply-add under walk, and hand-written products are no faster: at 16K multiply-adds (64x16 . 16x16), 217 ms library, 215 ms juxtaposition, 383 ms a `fill` of `SUM`s, 325 ms a sequential loop | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | design limit (walk) | silent | none | run-c | At 65K, (64x64 . 64x16) is 884 ms library against 1259 ms as a loop, (64x16 . (64x16)^T) 890 against 1098 ms; second of two repetitions, the first up to 2x slower; about 13 us per multiply-add here, Run C's 24 us a uniform host factor. The library product is the fastest spelling, a loop 1.4-1.5x slower, and cost tracks the multiply-add count, not the shape: the library's recursive partition costs per leaf what a `for` costs per iteration. A batch-1 step is about 190K multiply-adds plus the row-wise maps; the check's finite differences, two steps per index, take 855 s at one thread.
